REGULATORY AUTHORITY/PERMIT ACTION\n\nIn November 1972, Congress passed the Federal Water Pollution Control Act establishing the\nNPDES permit program to control water pollution. These amendments established technology-\nbased or end-of-pipe control mechanisms and an interim goal to achieve “water quality which\nprovides for the protection and propagation of fish, shellfish, and wildlife and provides for\nrecreation in and on the water,” more commonly known as the “swimmable, fishable” goal.\nFurther amendments in 1977 of the CWA gave EPA the authority to implement pollution control\nprograms, such as setting wastewater standards for industry, and established the basic structure\nfor regulating pollutants discharges into the waters of the United States. In addition, it made it\nunlawful for any person to discharge any pollutant from a point source into navigable waters,\nunless a permit was obtained under its’ provisions. Regulations governing the EPA administered\nNPDES permit program are generally found at 40 CFR §122 (program requirements & permit\nconditions), §124 (procedures for decision making), §125 (technology-based standards) and §136\n(analytical procedures). Other parts of 40 CFR provide guidance for specific activities and may\nbe used in this document as required.\n\nThe permittee’s requests are discussed below. It is proposed that the permit be reissued for a 5-\nyear term following regulations promulgated at 40 CFR §122.46(a).\n\nV.
